Instructions
Scientific tasks protocol v2 — human-in-the-loop scientific workflow.

1. Formulate an appropriate mathematical model yourself. Separate facts supplied by the user from choices or extra assumptions you introduce. Prefer deriving or estimating model parameters from the supplied observations and, when useful, requested experiments. State and justify the model family and observation model needed for that inference. For example, assume and justify a distribution family explicitly, then derive its parameters from the supplied moments, observations or experiment results; do not assume both the family and a complete set of parameter values when those values are what the task requires you to infer. Do not choose convenient parameter values and merely show that they reproduce the observations, presenting that exercise as inference. If the observations do not determine the parameters, expose what remains undetermined and what additional information or assumptions would be needed. Do not silently turn population moments into a fully specified prior. Do not assume the conclusion you are asked to establish as a premise.
2. If the information does not determine a unique answer, explain why and give a conditional answer with its assumptions exposed. You may revise the model and conclusion across attempts; preserve the reasons and lineage.
3. Write a substantive Lean proof covering the scientific inference needed to answer the question, connecting the supplied information and explicit modeling choices to the claimed result. Use Bayesian inference where appropriate; it is not mandatory for every task. A proof of arithmetic for a number calculated elsewhere is insufficient. The human will assess scientific relevance and whether the proof covers the actual inference.
4. Use available tools, including Internet research. Request literature, calculations, experiments or clarification from the operator when useful; a request is optional. Use the request tool below, stop for a real response, and incorporate the response with its source and limitations. Never invent an external response.
5. Run the actual check tool. Read and fix its real errors, retrying without replacing science with a smaller unrelated theorem. Preserve model changes and raw traces.
6. You may introduce additional premises or axioms, but expose them for review. Any sorry or additional axiom is conditional, never an unconditional full pass. Lean correctness does not establish the truth of the premises.
7. Extract definitions, constants, data, assumptions and the exact conclusions. The harness independently compiles and compares the extracted statements and runs a separate kernel check. A failed or unknown check is not success.
8. A human reviews the actual content and may accept or reject it with feedback. If the researcher rejects the assumptions or conclusions, revise your model, assumptions, conclusions and proof in response to the feedback. Never accept your own result or claim human approval.

Human-readability requirements:
- Write the formalization so a human scientist can understand what is assumed and what is concluded, with a concise plain-language translation alongside the relevant Lean statements. Explain the real-world meaning of the model and the reason for each scientific modeling choice.
- Keep the assumptions, scientifically meaningful definitions and final conclusions as small and direct as possible. Avoid redundant definitions, duplicate specializations of the same formula, synonyms, and layers of wrappers that merely rename an expression. Retain a helper definition only when it materially improves clarity or is mathematically necessary.
- Put derivable facts and technical work into intermediate lemmas or proof-local helpers: algebra, normalization, parameter calculations and other consequences should be proved rather than added to the review assumptions. Keep the final conclusion focused on the actual scientific answer.
- Minimizing the review boundary must preserve its meaning: expose every independent modeling choice and every definition needed to interpret the conclusion. Do not hide assumptions in intermediate lemmas, use unproved facts as lemmas, or shorten the conclusion into an unexplained proposition alias.
- Before submission, simplify the public statement and its dependency chain, and give a compact review section separating supplied information, additional assumptions, necessary definitions and proved conclusions. Definitions introduced solely for proof organization should stay inside the proof when possible. Do not optimize for a short file at the cost of a longer or less intelligible assumptions/conclusions packet.

Isolation rule: work only from this task, these instructions, native installed libraries, and the run's own history and supplied responses. Do not seek previous solutions, task-specific proofs, or preset model families, parameter values or expected answers from earlier runs. You may consult general scientific or statistical literature to select and justify a model family. Authentication metadata is mounted only because the native subscription client needs its existing login; it is not scientific evidence. Do not read, print, copy or modify authentication files. Never use paid APIs, API-key fallback, other agents, or external services requiring payment. Stop on subscription quota/authentication errors.

Tools (run in the terminal):
  python /harness/src/vcs_protocol.py tool check --manifest /work/candidate.json
  python /harness/src/vcs_protocol.py tool request --kind literature --question '...'
Kinds are literature, calculation, experiment, clarification. A request stops this cycle; the operator supplies an answer and resumes. No physical measurement device is connected. You may request a real measurement; do not report an unperformed measurement. Any synthetic calculation or simulated experiment must be explicitly labeled as such and is evidence about its chosen model, not a measurement of the physical system. If no measurement is available, provide a scientifically useful conditional prediction or range with the missing information and assumptions stated, rather than claiming an unconditional numerical answer. Record incorporated response IDs in the candidate. Evidence is user/external supplied and not guaranteed true.

Write /work/candidate.json with this shape (all paths relative to /work):
{"proof_file":"Model.lean", "narrative_file":"report.txt", "conclusions":{"answer":"answer_theorem"}, "user_facts":["..."], "model_choices":["..."], "sources":["..."], "incorporated_inputs":[], "proof_scope":"What the theorems actually establish and what is left to human review"}
List only the final scientific conclusion or conclusions needed to answer the task, with nonempty labels mapped to actual, distinct Lean theorem declarations. A single answer theorem is allowed. Supporting model, inference, normalization and other derived facts may be intermediate lemmas; there are no mandatory model/update/prediction conclusion roles. Use plain top-level `theorem name : statement := proof` declarations, with all binders inside `statement`. Imports must be explicit `import Mathlib.…` lines at the start of the file; imports of project/task files are unavailable. The restricted proof language supports definitions, structures, lemmas, axioms and sorry but no executable metaprogramming, custom syntax, namespaces, native_decide, IO or unsafe code. Check feedback gives concrete unsupported syntax errors. Native Mathlib tactics are available. Explain any restriction that prevents a scientifically meaningful formalization; do not pretend a narrower result meets the task.
Task
I have a cup of coffee initially at 90 degrees Celsius and want to know when it will cool to 60 degrees Celsius. Choose a reasonable cup shape and size yourself, explicitly identifying these as chosen scenario details rather than measured facts. You may request measurements of temperature, cup geometry or surrounding conditions. No time-temperature measurements or cooling coefficients have yet been supplied. How long will the coffee take to cool to 60 degrees Celsius?
assistant
I’ll give a conditional cooling-time prediction, making the chosen cup and room conditions explicit. I’ll also formalize why the two supplied temperatures alone cannot determine a cooling time, then run the required Lean check.

"""Restricted Lean validation and kernel-environment assumption audit.

Stable VAS_ audit markers preserve compatibility with recorded evidence.
"""
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
_TOOLCHAIN = (ROOT / "lean-toolchain").read_text().strip().replace("/", "--").replace(":", "---")
LEAN = os.environ.get("VAS_LEAN") or str(Path(os.environ.get("ELAN_HOME", Path.home() / ".elan")) / "toolchains" / _TOOLCHAIN / "bin/lean")
LEANCHECKER = str(Path(LEAN).with_name("leanchecker"))
ALLOWED_AXIOMS = {"propext", "Classical.choice", "Quot.sound"}
FORBIDDEN = re.compile(
    r"\b(axioms|native_decide|implemented_by|extern|unsafe|opaque|import|"
    r"macro|macro_rules|syntax|elab|notation|infix|infixl|infixr|prefix|postfix|"
    r"run_cmd|run_tac|run_elab|run_meta|initialize|builtin_initialize|instance|attribute|"
    r"partial|private|local|scoped|namespace|section|end|universe|variable|export|IO|System|"
    r"Lean|native|simproc|dsimproc)\b"
    # any word containing elab (by_elab, elab_rules, run_elab: `\belab\b` misses them, `_` is a word character);
    # backtick name literals and quotations are metaprogramming, which a proof body never needs (OT-1861)
    r"|\w*elab\w*|`|#|@\[|\$|«|»")


def strip_comments(source):
    out, i, depth = [], 0, 0
    while i < len(source):
        if source.startswith("/-", i):
            depth += 1
            i += 2
        elif depth and source.startswith("-/", i):
            depth -= 1
            i += 2
        elif depth:
            i += 1
        elif source.startswith("--", i):
            j = source.find("\n", i)
            i = len(source) if j == -1 else j
            out.append("\n")
        else:
            out.append(source[i])
            i += 1
    if depth:
        raise ValueError("Unclosed Lean comment")
    return "".join(out)


def validate(source, required):
    if not isinstance(source, str) or not 0 < len(source.encode()) <= 24000:
        raise ValueError("Lean body must contain at most 24000 UTF-8 bytes")
    code = strip_comments(source)
    if '"' in code:
        raise ValueError("Put prose in comments; executable strings are outside this contract")
    code = re.sub(r"^\s*set_option (maxRecDepth|maxHeartbeats) \d+\s*$", "", code, flags=re.M)
    if re.search(r"\bset_option\b", code):
        raise ValueError("Only maxRecDepth/maxHeartbeats options are available")
    if "VAS_" in code:
        raise ValueError("Names containing VAS_ are reserved for the checker's own report")
    bad = FORBIDDEN.search(code)
    if bad:
        raise ValueError(f"Outside the restricted Lean contract: {bad.group()}")
    if re.search(r"\b(?:def|abbrev|theorem|lemma|structure|inductive|class)\s+[^\s:(]+\.", code):
        raise ValueError("Declare plain top-level names; namespaces are outside this contract")
    theorems = re.findall(r"^\s*(?:theorem|lemma)\s+([A-Za-z_][A-Za-z_0-9']*)", code, re.M)
    missing = [n for n in required if n not in theorems]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"Missing required theorems: {missing}")
    if re.search(r"\baxiom\b(?!\s+[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z_0-9']*\s*[:({\[])", code):
        raise ValueError("Declare an added assumption as `axiom name : statement` at the start of a line")
    return theorems


# Lean itself lists what the proof adds: every axiom declared in the file and every sorry (by its elaborated goal with
# the hypotheses in scope), each marked used when a conclusion theorem reaches it. Read from the checked kernel
# environment, as #print axioms does. Never part of an agent body (run_cmd is outside the contract).
LISTER = """
/-- true if `sorryAx` occurs anywhere other than as the head of an application that states its goal -/
private partial def VAS_unstated : Lean.Expr → Bool
  | e@(.app ..) =>
    let f := e.getAppFn
    (if f.isConstOf ``sorryAx then false else VAS_unstated f) || e.getAppArgs.any VAS_unstated
  | .const n _ => n == ``sorryAx
  | .lam _ t b _ | .forallE _ t b _ => VAS_unstated t || VAS_unstated b
  | .letE _ t v b _ => VAS_unstated t || VAS_unstated v || VAS_unstated b
  | .mdata _ b | .proj _ _ b => VAS_unstated b
  | _ => false

set_option format.width 100000 in
set_option pp.deepTerms true in
set_option pp.proofs true in
set_option pp.maxSteps 100000000 in
open Lean Elab Command Meta in
run_cmd do
  let kenv := (← getEnv).checked.get
  let locals := kenv.constants.map₂.toList
  let localSet : NameSet := locals.foldl (fun s (n, _) => s.insert n) {}
  let exprsOf (ci : ConstantInfo) : Array Expr :=
    #[ci.type] ++ (match ci with
      | .defnInfo v => #[v.value] | .thmInfo v => #[v.value] | .opaqueInfo v => #[v.value] | _ => #[])
  let mut used : NameSet := {}
  let mut todo : List Name := ROOTS
  while !todo.isEmpty do
    let n := todo.head!
    todo := todo.tail!
    if used.contains n then continue
    used := used.insert n
    if let some ci := kenv.find? n then
      for e in exprsOf ci do
        for c in e.getUsedConstants do
          if localSet.contains c && !used.contains c then todo := c :: todo
  for (n, ci) in locals do
    if let .axiomInfo _ := ci then
      logInfo m!"VAS_AXIOM {n} used={used.contains n} : {← liftTermElabM <| ppExpr ci.type} VAS_END"
    -- every declaration kind, type and value: a gap may sit in an axiom's type as well as in a proof
    for e in exprsOf ci do
      -- hasSorry is cached and linear; VAS_unstated walks the term as a tree, exponential on shared proofs
      if e.hasSorry && VAS_unstated e then
        logInfo m!"VAS_GAP {n} used={used.contains n} : (sorry with no stated goal: it can assert anything) VAS_END"
        logInfo m!"VAS_UNSTATED {n} used={used.contains n}"
    if (exprsOf ci).any (·.hasSorry) then
      let seen ← IO.mkRef ({} : Std.HashSet String)
      liftTermElabM do
        for e in exprsOf ci do
          forEachExpr' e fun sub => do
            if sub.isAppOf ``sorryAx && sub.getAppNumArgs ≥ 1 then
              let mut t ← instantiateMVars (sub.getArg! 0)
              if t.isForall && t.bindingDomain!.isConstOf ``Lean.Name then t := t.bindingBody!
              let hyps ← (← getLCtx).foldlM (init := m!"") fun acc d => do
                if d.isImplementationDetail then return acc
                match d.value? with
                | some v => return acc ++ m!"({d.userName} : {← ppExpr d.type} := {← ppExpr v}) "
                | none => return acc ++ m!"({d.userName} : {← ppExpr d.type}) "
              let line ← (m!"VAS_GAP {n} used={used.contains n} : {hyps}⊢ {← ppExpr t} VAS_END").toString
              unless (← seen.get).contains line do
                seen.modify (·.insert line)
                logInfo line
            return true
  logInfo "VAS_LISTED"
"""


def listed(text):
    """Parse the lister's output into added_assumptions entries; None if the lister did not finish."""
    if not re.search(r"^VAS_LISTED\s*$", text, re.M):  # the marker line itself, not a substring of other output
        return None
    out = []
    for kind, name, used, stmt in re.findall(r"VAS_(AXIOM|GAP) (\S+) used=(true|false) : (.*?) VAS_END", text, re.S):
        out.append({"kind": kind.lower(), "in" if kind == "GAP" else "name": name, "used": used == "true",
                    "statement": " ".join(stmt.split())})
    return out

  "command": "/usr/bin/bash -lc \"cat > /work/Model.lean <<'EOF'\nimport Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Basic\nimport Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.ExpDeriv\nimport Mathlib.Tactic\n\n/- Chosen deterministic scenario: a uniformly mixed coffee in a constant 20 C\nroom. Time t is in minutes; k is an UNKNOWN effective loss rate in min\"'^-1.\nThe exponential family is the modeling assumption, not measured evidence.\nThe factor 70 is the supplied initial 90 C minus the chosen ambient 20 C. -/\ndef coffee (k t : ℝ) : ℝ := 20 + 70 * Real.exp (-k * t)\n\n-- Every rate fits the supplied initial temperature; it contains no rate information.\nlemma initial_temperature : ∀ k : ℝ, coffee k 0 = 90 := by\n  intro k\n  norm_num [coffee]\n\n-- The chosen family satisfies Newton'\"'s cooling equation, with no extra premise.\nlemma cooling_equation : ∀ k t : ℝ,\n    HasDerivAt (coffee k) (-k * (coffee k t - 20)) t := by\n  intro k t\n  have h := (((hasDerivAt_id t).const_mul (-k)).exp.const_mul 70).const_add 20\n  convert h using 1 <;> simp [coffee] <;> ring\n\nlemma threshold_exponential :\n    Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by\n  have h : Real.log (4 / 7 : ℝ) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by\n    rw [← Real.log_inv]\n    norm_num\n  rw [← h, Real.exp_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 4 / 7)]\n\n-- Solving the observation equation: 60 C occurs at exactly log(7/4)/k minutes.\nlemma threshold_time : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,\n    (coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) := by\n  intro k hk t\n  constructor\n  · intro h\n    have he : Real.exp (-k * t) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by\n      dsimp [coffee] at h\n      linarith\n    have hx : -k * t = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) :=\n      Real.exp_injective (he.trans threshold_exponential.symm)\n    apply (eq_div_iff (ne_of_gt hk)).2\n    nlinarith\n  · intro h\n    rw [h]\n    have hx : -k * (Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by\n      field_simp\n    unfold coffee\n    rw [hx, threshold_exponential]\n    norm_num\n\n-- At or below the target iff the threshold time has passed: this is a first crossing.\nlemma threshold_order : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,\n    (coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t) := by\n  intro k hk t\n  calc\n    coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.exp (-k * t) ≤ Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) := by\n      rw [threshold_exponential]\n      unfold coffee\n      constructor <;> intro h <;> linarith\n    _ ↔ -k * t ≤ -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.exp_le_exp\n    _ ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t := by\n      rw [div_le_iff₀ hk]\n      constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith\n\n/- Scientific answer, conditional on the family above:\n(1) Every positive k fits 90 C initially, predicts a positive first hitting time\n    log(7/4)/k, and is above 60 C before that time.\n(2) For EVERY proposed positive time tau, some positive k gives exactly that\n    first hitting time while fitting the same supplied initial temperature.\nThus these data cannot identify even a bounded positive cooling time. -/\ntheorem answer_theorem :\n    (∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k →\n      coffee k 0 = 90 ∧\n      0 < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ∧\n      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) ∧\n      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t)) ∧\n    (∀ tau : ℝ, 0 < tau → ∃ k : ℝ, 0 < k ∧ coffee k 0 = 90 ∧\n      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = tau) ∧\n      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ tau ≤ t)) := by\n  have hL : 0 < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.log_pos (by norm_num)\n  constructor\n  · intro k hk\n    exact ⟨initial_temperature k, div_pos hL hk, threshold_time k hk,\n      threshold_order k hk⟩\n  · intro tau htau\n    let k := Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / tau\n    have hk : 0 < k := div_pos hL htau\n    have htime : Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k = tau := by\n      dsimp [k]\n      field_simp\n    refine ⟨k, hk, initial_temperature k, ?_, ?_⟩\n    · intro t\n      rw [threshold_time k hk, htime]\n    · intro t\n      rw [threshold_order k hk, htime]\nEOF\ncat > /work/report.txt <<'EOF'\nThe supplied information does not determine a cooling time.\n\nCompact review packet\nSupplied information: initial coffee temperature 90 degrees Celsius; desired target 60 degrees Celsius. No ambient temperature, elapsed-temperature reading, or heat-transfer coefficient is supplied. The target is not an observed timed event.\n\nChosen scenario details, not measurements: an open cylindrical ceramic mug of internal radius 4 cm, filled to depth 5 cm (about 251 mL), in a room held at 20 degrees Celsius. These describe a reasonable cup but do not determine its heat loss. Cup thickness, thermal state, contact with a table, humidity, air movement and evaporation remain unspecified. Geometry is descriptive context and has no numerical role in the conditional inference; the effective cooling rate is left free rather than assigned from geometry.\n\nIndependent modeling choices: represent the liquid by a single uniform temperature (e.g. gently mixed); hold ambient at 20 C; approximate total heat loss over 90 to 60 C by a positive, constant effective Newton cooling rate k in inverse minutes, with no additions or active heating. This is a deterministic exponential family, coffee(k,t) = 20 + 70 exp(-k t), for physical times t >= 0. Its algebraic extension to all real times is used in the statements. There is no prior distribution and no assumed rate value. The supplied initial temperature is treated as exact, not as a noisy reading. With only that initial reading there is no observation error distribution to fit and no information about k.\n\nReason for model: a lumped thermal energy balance C dT/dt = -G(T-20) with constant effective thermal capacitance C and conductance G gives k = G/C (with units converted to minutes). Neither G nor C is inferred here. COMSOL's primary documentation explains the homogeneous-temperature, constant-coefficient lumped approximation and its limitations for solids: https://doc.comsol.com/6.3/doc/com.comsol.help.heat/heat_ug_theory.07.039.html (accessed 2026-11-16). Applying a single-temperature effective model to this mixed liquid is our additional assumption, not a conclusion of that source. The proof verifies that the chosen exponential family satisfies dT/dt = -k(T-20). It does not prove that the real mug obeys the approximation. Radiation, evaporation and cup thermal transients can make a constant k inaccurate. No physical measurement or simulation has been performed.\n\nNecessary definition: coffee(k,t) is the temperature in Celsius in the chosen model. The 70 C excess is derived from 90 minus the chosen 20 C ambient. Natural logarithm and exponential are Mathlib real functions. Cup dimensions are not hidden parameters in this definition.\n\nProved conclusion (answer_theorem): for every k > 0, the model fits 90 C at time zero and first reaches 60 C at the positive time t60 = ln(7/4)/k. In addition, for every positive proposed time tau, choosing k = ln(7/4)/tau yields a model with the same initial temperature and exactly that first target crossing. All times before the crossing are above 60 C, and all times after it are at or below 60 C. Hence the initial datum alone permits any positive time within this model family; there is no identifiable numerical answer or finite positive range. This nonidentifiability is already present with ambient fixed at the chosen 20 C. It persists when ambient is also unknown.\n\nPractical conditional prediction: t60 = ln(70/40)/k, approximately 0.5596/k minutes if k is expressed in inverse minutes. The decimal is explanatory only; the formal answer uses the exact logarithm. The theorem is about the model and does not assert that arbitrarily short or long times are physically attainable in the chosen ceramic mug. No independent physical bounds on its effective k have been supplied or derived.\n\nWhat would determine a prediction: measure actual ambient a and one coffee temperature y after a known elapsed time s > 0, keeping the same cup and conditions. import hashlib
import json
import os
from pathlib import Path
import re
import subprocess
import tempfile
import sys

import click

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
from vcs_lean import LEAN, LEANCHECKER, LISTER, listed  # noqa: E402

# Display text omits implicit arguments, types and sometimes whole subterms.
# Compare the complete kernel expressions instead. Binder names and metadata do
# not affect meaning; remove them so source positions and fresh binder names do
# not make an otherwise identical extraction fail. All constants, universe
# levels, implicit arguments and binder information remain in the identity.
IDENTITY = """
private partial def VAS_identityExpr : Lean.Expr → Lean.Expr
  | .mdata _ value => VAS_identityExpr value
  | .app fn arg => .app (VAS_identityExpr fn) (VAS_identityExpr arg)
  | .lam _ typ body info => .lam .anonymous (VAS_identityExpr typ) (VAS_identityExpr body) info
  | .forallE _ typ body info => .forallE .anonymous (VAS_identityExpr typ) (VAS_identityExpr body) info
  | .letE _ typ value body flag => .letE .anonymous (VAS_identityExpr typ) (VAS_identityExpr value) (VAS_identityExpr body) flag
  | .proj owner idx value => .proj owner idx (VAS_identityExpr value)
  | value => value

private def VAS_declarationIdentity (ci : Lean.ConstantInfo) : String :=
  let kind := match ci with
    | .axiomInfo _ => "axiom"
    | .defnInfo _ => "definition"
    | .thmInfo _ => "theorem"
    | .opaqueInfo _ => "opaque"
    | .quotInfo _ => "quotient"
    | .inductInfo info => s!"inductive {info.numParams} {info.numIndices} {reprStr info.ctors}"
    | .ctorInfo info => s!"constructor {info.induct} {info.cidx} {info.numParams} {info.numFields}"
    | .recInfo info => s!"recursor {info.numParams} {info.numIndices} {info.numMotives} {info.numMinors}"
  let value := match ci.value? with
    | some body => reprStr (VAS_identityExpr body)
    | none => "no value"
  s!"{kind}|levels={reprStr ci.levelParams}|type={reprStr (VAS_identityExpr ci.type)}|value={value}"
"""

REACH = """
open Lean Elab Command Meta in
run_cmd do
  let kenv := (← getEnv).checked.get
  let localSet : NameSet := kenv.constants.map₂.toList.foldl (fun s (n, _) => s.insert n) {}
  -- statements only: from each conclusion's type, through definitions' types and values
  let mut seen : NameSet := {}
  let mut todo : List Name := []
  for t in TARGETS do
    let some ci := kenv.find? t | throwError m!"no theorem {t}"
    todo := ci.type.getUsedConstants.toList ++ todo
    logInfo m!"VAS_CONCLUSION {t} : {← liftTermElabM <| ppExpr ci.type} VAS_END"
    logInfo m!"VAS_ORIGINAL_IDENTITY {t} : {reprStr (VAS_identityExpr ci.type)} VAS_END"
  while !todo.isEmpty do
    let n := todo.head!
    todo := todo.tail!
    if seen.contains n || !localSet.contains n then continue
    seen := seen.insert n
    let some ci := kenv.find? n | continue
    logInfo m!"VAS_DEPENDENCY_IDENTITY {n} : {VAS_declarationIdentity ci} VAS_END"
    let more := ci.type.getUsedConstants ++ (match ci with
      | .defnInfo v => v.value.getUsedConstants | .thmInfo v => v.value.getUsedConstants
      | .opaqueInfo v => v.value.getUsedConstants
      | .inductInfo v => v.ctors.toArray | .ctorInfo v => #[v.induct] | _ => #[])
    todo := more.toList ++ todo
    -- auxiliary constants (matchers, constructors, projections, equation lemmas) map to the nearest ancestor
    -- whose source range Lean recorded
    let mut m := n
    let mut r ← findDeclarationRanges? m
    while r.isNone && !m.isAnonymous do
      m := m.getPrefix
      r ← findDeclarationRanges? m
    match r with
    | some rg => logInfo m!"VAS_DECL {m} {rg.range.pos.line} {rg.range.endPos.line}"
    | none => logInfo m!"VAS_UNRANGED {n}"
  logInfo "VAS_REACHED"
"""

COMPARE = """
set_option format.width 100000 in
open Lean Elab Command Meta in
run_cmd do
  for n in NAMES do
    let some ci := (← getEnv).find? n | throwError m!"no {n}"
    let some v := ci.value? | throwError m!"no value {n}"
    logInfo m!"VAS_EXTRACTED {n} : {← liftTermElabM <| ppExpr v} VAS_END"
    logInfo m!"VAS_EXTRACTED_IDENTITY {n} : {reprStr (VAS_identityExpr v)} VAS_END"
  for n in DEPENDENCIES do
    let some ci := (← getEnv).find? n | throwError m!"missing extracted dependency {n}"
    logInfo m!"VAS_EXTRACTED_DEPENDENCY_IDENTITY {n} : {VAS_declarationIdentity ci} VAS_END"
"""


def run_lean(path):
    """Lean, then leanchecker on the compiled module (OT-1861): a nonzero status if either fails."""
    libs = sorted((ROOT / ".lake/packages").glob("*/.lake/build/lib/lean"))
    env = dict(os.environ, LEAN_PATH=":".join(map(str, libs)), LEAN_NUM_THREADS="1")
    with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(prefix="vcs-olean-") as build:
        r = subprocess.run([LEAN, "-j1", f"--root={Path(path).resolve().parent}", "-o", str(Path(build) / "VasExtract.olean"), str(path)], cwd=ROOT,
                           env=env, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=900)
        if r.returncode != 0:
            return r.returncode, r.stdout + r.stderr
        c = subprocess.run([LEANCHECKER, "VasExtract"], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=900,
                           env=dict(env, LEAN_PATH=env["LEAN_PATH"] + ":" + build,
                                    PATH=str(Path(LEAN).parent) + ":" + env.get("PATH", "")))
        note = f"\nleanchecker exit {c.returncode}\n{(c.stdout + c.stderr)[-1500:]}\n"
        return (0 if c.returncode == 0 else 10), r.stdout + r.stderr + note


def expression_identities(text, marker):
    """Read complete expression records; duplicate/missing records fail closed."""
    records = re.findall(r"^" + re.escape(marker) + r" (\S+) : (.*?) VAS_END", text, re.M | re.S)
    result = {}
    for name, value in records:
        if name in result:
            raise ValueError(f"duplicate expression identity for {name}")
        result[name] = " ".join(value.split())
    return result


def statement_text(lines, start, name):
    """The source of theorem `name`'s statement: from after `name :` to the `:=` that opens its proof."""
    text = "\n".join(lines[start - 1:])
    m = re.match(r"\s*(?:theorem|lemma)\s+" + re.escape(name) + r"\s*:(.*?):=", text, re.S)
    if not m:
        raise ValueError(f"cannot read the statement of {name}")
    return " ".join(m.group(1).split())


@click.command()
@click.argument("lean_file", type=click.Path(exists=True, dir_okay=False))
@click.option("--theorem", "theorems", multiple=True, required=True, help="a conclusion theorem (repeatable)")
@click.option("--out", "out_stem", required=True, help="output stem: writes <out>.lean and <out>.json")
def main(lean_file, theorems, out_stem):
    src = Path(lean_file).read_text()
    lines = src.splitlines()
    out = Path(out_stem)
    probe = out.with_name(out.name + ".probe.lean")
    targets = "[" + ", ".join(f"``{t}" for t in theorems) + "]"
    body = re.sub(r"(?m)^#print axioms.*\n?", "", src)
    # a file written by the current checker already ends with its own LISTER; drop it, the probe appends one
    head = next(ln for ln in LISTER.splitlines() if ln.strip())
    if head in body:
        body = body[:body.index(head)]
    probe.write_text(body + "\nset_option format.width 100000\n" + IDENTITY + REACH.replace("TARGETS", targets)
                     + LISTER.replace("ROOTS", targets))
    status, text = run_lean(probe)
    report = {"file": lean_file, "conclusions": list(theorems), "lean_exit": status}
    if status != 0 or "VAS_REACHED" not in text:
        report |= {"ok": False, "error": "the source did not compile with the extractor",
                   "errors": [ln for ln in text.splitlines() if ": error" in ln][:5]}
        print(json.dumps(report, indent=1))
        sys.exit(1)
    decls = {}
    for name, a, b in re.findall(r"VAS_DECL (\S+) (\d+) (\d+)", text):
        decls[name] = (int(a), int(b))
    unranged = re.findall(r"VAS_UNRANGED (\S+)", text)
    concl = dict(re.findall(r"VAS_CONCLUSION (\S+) : (.*?) VAS_END", text, re.S))
    original_identities = expression_identities(text, "VAS_ORIGINAL_IDENTITY")
    dependency_identities = expression_identities(text, "VAS_DEPENDENCY_IDENTITY")
    added = [a for a in listed(text) or [] if a["used"]]
    # the extracted file: imports, then each assumption's source in file order, inside its namespace. A range
    # inside another one (a structure's fields and constructor) is part of it and is not copied twice.
    imports = [ln for ln in lines if ln.startswith("import ")]
    spans = sorted(set(decls.values()))
    keep = [s for s in spans if not any(o != s and o[0] <= s[0] and s[1] <= o[1] for o in spans)]
    for x, y in zip(keep, keep[1:]):
        if y[0] <= x[1]:
            raise ValueError(f"overlapping declaration ranges {x} and {y}")
    owner = {}
    for name, rng in decls.items():
        if rng in keep and (rng not in owner or len(name) < len(owner[rng])):
            owner[rng] = name
    head = re.compile(r"^\s*(?:@\[[^\]]*\]\s*)?(?:noncomputable\s+|private\s+|protected\s+)*"
                      r"(?:def|theorem|lemma|abbrev|structure|inductive|class|axiom|instance|opaque)\s+([^\s:({\[]+)")
    # top-level `open X` lines of the source go along, in file order, so names resolve as they did there
    opens = [(i + 1, i + 1) for i, ln in enumerate(lines)
             if re.match(r"open\s+[\w.]+(\s+[\w.]+)*\s*$", ln) and not any(a <= i + 1 <= b for a, b in keep)]
    parts, current = [], None
    for rng in sorted(keep + opens):
        a, b = rng
        if rng in opens and rng not in owner:
            if current:
                parts.append(f"end {current}\n")
                current = None
            parts.append(lines[a - 1])
            continue
        name = owner[rng]
        short = next((m.group(1) for ln in lines[a - 1:b] if (m := head.match(ln))), None)
        if short is None or not (name == short or name.endswith("." + short)):
            raise ValueError(f"cannot match the source of {name} at lines {a}-{b}")
        ns = name[: -len(short) - 1] if name != short else ""
        if ns != current:
            if current:
                parts.append(f"end {current}\n")
            if ns:
                parts.append(f"namespace {ns}")
            current = ns
        parts.append("\n".join(lines[a - 1:b]) + "\n")
    if current:
        parts.append(f"end {current}\n")
    starts = {}
    for t in theorems:
        m = re.search(r"(?m)^\s*(?:theorem|lemma)\s+" + re.escape(t) + r"\b", src)
        starts[t] = src[:m.start()].count("\n") + 1 if m else None
    stmts = {t: statement_text(lines, starts[t], t) for t in theorems}
    conclusions = "".join(f"def conclusion_{t} : Prop := {stmts[t]}\n" for t in theorems)
    extracted = ("\n".join(imports) + "\n\n" + "\n".join(parts) + "\n\n"
                 "/-! Conclusions (statements of the proved theorems). -/\n" + conclusions)
    out.with_suffix(".lean").write_text(extracted)
    # step 5: the extracted file compiles alone, and its conclusions are the proved statements
    check = out.with_name(out.name + ".check.lean")
    names = "[" + ", ".join(f"``conclusion_{t}" for t in theorems) + "]"
    dependencies = "[" + ", ".join(f"``{name}" for name in dependency_identities) + "]"
    check.write_text(extracted + IDENTITY + COMPARE.replace("NAMES", names).replace("DEPENDENCIES", dependencies))
    status2, text2 = run_lean(check)
    extracted_identities = expression_identities(text2, "VAS_EXTRACTED_IDENTITY")
    same = {t: bool(original_identities.get(t)) and
            original_identities[t] == extracted_identities.get("conclusion_" + t)
            for t in theorems}
    extracted_dependencies = expression_identities(text2, "VAS_EXTRACTED_DEPENDENCY_IDENTITY")
    dependencies_same = {name: value == extracted_dependencies.get(name)
                         for name, value in dependency_identities.items()}
    identity_hashes = {
        t: {"original_sha256": hashlib.sha256(original_identities[t].encode()).hexdigest()
            if t in original_identities else None,
            "extracted_sha256": hashlib.sha256(extracted_identities["conclusion_" + t].encode()).hexdigest()
            if "conclusion_" + t in extracted_identities else None}
        for t in theorems}
    dependency_hashes = {
        name: {"original_sha256": hashlib.sha256(value.encode()).hexdigest(),
               "extracted_sha256": hashlib.sha256(extracted_dependencies[name].encode()).hexdigest()
               if name in extracted_dependencies else None}
        for name, value in dependency_identities.items()}
    report |= {"ok": status2 == 0 and all(same.values()) and all(dependencies_same.values())
               and set(dependency_identities) == set(extracted_dependencies) and not unranged,
               "compiles_alone": status2 == 0,
               "faithful": same, "assumption_declarations": [owner[r] for r in keep],
               "identity_method": "kernel-expression-repr-v1", "conclusion_identities": identity_hashes,
               "dependency_faithful": dependencies_same, "dependency_identities": dependency_hashes,
               "unranged": unranged, "added_assumptions": added,
               "conclusions_as_proved": {t: " ".join(concl[t].split()) for t in theorems},
               "extracted_file": str(out.with_suffix(".lean")),
               "errors": [ln for ln in text2.splitlines() if ": error" in ln][:5]}

                (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.NF.eval_mul_eval_cons_zero (one_mul (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.NF.eval []))
                  (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.NF.eval_cons_of_pow_eq_zero (of_decide_eq_true (id (Eq.refl true)))
                    (ne_of_gt
                      (Mathlib.Meta.Positivity.log_pos_of_isNNRat
                        (Mathlib.Meta.NormNum.isNNRat_div
                          (Mathlib.Meta.NormNum.isNNRat_mul (Eq.refl HMul.hMul)
                            (Mathlib.Meta.NormNum.IsNat.to_isNNRat (Mathlib.Meta.NormNum.isNat_ofNat ℝ (Eq.refl 7)))
                            (Mathlib.Meta.NormNum.isNNRat_inv_pos
                              (Mathlib.Meta.NormNum.IsNat.to_isNNRat (Mathlib.Meta.NormNum.isNat_ofNat ℝ (Eq.refl 4))))
                            (Eq.refl (Nat.mul 7 1)) (Eq.refl 4)))
                        (Eq.refl true)))
                    [])))))
          rfl (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.eq_div_of_eq_one_of_subst (Eq.symm (div_one 1)) rfl))
        (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.eq_mul_of_eq_eq_eq_mul rfl
          (Eq.trans (Eq.trans (Eq.refl tau) (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.NF.atom_eq_eval tau))
            (Eq.symm
              (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.NF.eval_cons_mul_eval 1 tau (one_mul (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.NF.eval [])))))
          rfl (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.eq_div_of_eq_one_of_subst (Eq.symm (div_one 1)) rfl))
        (Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp.NF.cons_ne_zero 1 (ne_of_gt htau) one_ne_zero))
      (eq_self 1)))) ⊢ ∀ (t : ℝ), coffee k t ≤ ↑60 ↔ tau ≤ t VAS_END

assistant
**The supplied information cannot determine a cooling time.**

I chose an open cylindrical ceramic mug, 8 cm across with 5 cm coffee depth (about 251 mL), in a constant 20°C room. These are scenario assumptions, not measurements.

Assuming uniform temperature and Newton cooling,

\[
T(t)=20+70e^{-kt},
\qquad
t_{60}=\frac{\ln(7/4)}{k}\approx\frac{0.5596}{k}\text{ minutes}.
\]

The rate \(k\) remains unknown. The proof establishes that every positive target time is compatible with the initial temperature within this model. An ambient measurement and a timed coffee-temperature reading would allow estimating \(k\), conditional on the model.

The Lean, extraction, and independent kernel checks passed without added axioms or `sorry`. Scientific validity remains for human review.

[Report](/work/report.txt) · [Lean proof](/work/Model.lean) · [Candidate](/work/candidate.json)

Produced code

attempt-001/Model.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Basic
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.ExpDeriv
import Mathlib.Tactic.NormNum
import Mathlib.Tactic.Linarith
import Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp
import Mathlib.Tactic.Ring
import Mathlib.Tactic.Convert

/- Chosen deterministic scenario: a uniformly mixed coffee in a constant 20 C
room. Time t is in minutes; k is an UNKNOWN effective loss rate in min^-1.
The exponential family is the modeling assumption, not measured evidence.
The factor 70 is the supplied initial 90 C minus the chosen ambient 20 C. -/
noncomputable def coffee (k t : ℝ) : ℝ := 20 + 70 * Real.exp (-k * t)

-- Every rate fits the supplied initial temperature; it contains no rate information.
lemma initial_temperature : ∀ k : ℝ, coffee k 0 = 90 := by
  intro k
  norm_num [coffee]

-- The chosen family satisfies Newton's cooling equation, with no extra premise.
lemma cooling_equation : ∀ k t : ℝ,
    HasDerivAt (coffee k) (-k * (coffee k t - 20)) t := by
  intro k t
  have h := (((hasDerivAt_id t).const_mul (-k)).exp.const_mul 70).const_add 20
  change HasDerivAt (coffee k)
    (70 * (Real.exp (-k * t) * (-k * 1))) t at h
  convert h using 1 <;> dsimp [coffee] <;> ring

lemma threshold_exponential :
    Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
  have h : Real.log (4 / 7 : ℝ) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
    rw [← Real.log_inv]
    norm_num
  rw [← h, Real.exp_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 4 / 7)]

-- Solving the observation equation: 60 C occurs at exactly log(7/4)/k minutes.
lemma threshold_time : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) := by
  intro k hk t
  constructor
  · intro h
    have he : Real.exp (-k * t) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
      dsimp [coffee] at h
      linarith
    have hx : -k * t = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) :=
      Real.exp_injective (he.trans threshold_exponential.symm)
    apply (eq_div_iff (ne_of_gt hk)).2
    nlinarith
  · intro h
    rw [h]
    have hx : -k * (Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
      field_simp
    unfold coffee
    rw [hx, threshold_exponential]
    norm_num

-- At or below the target iff the threshold time has passed: this is a first crossing.
lemma threshold_order : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t) := by
  intro k hk t
  calc
    coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.exp (-k * t) ≤ Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) := by
      rw [threshold_exponential]
      unfold coffee
      constructor <;> intro h <;> linarith
    _ ↔ -k * t ≤ -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.exp_le_exp
    _ ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hk]
      constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

/- Scientific answer, conditional on the family above:
(1) Every positive k fits 90 C initially, predicts a positive first hitting time
    log(7/4)/k, and is above 60 C before that time.
(2) For EVERY proposed positive time tau, some positive k gives exactly that
    first hitting time while fitting the same supplied initial temperature.
Thus these data cannot identify even a bounded positive cooling time. -/
theorem answer_theorem :
    (∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k →
      coffee k 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      0 < Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k ≤ t)) ∧
    (∀ tau : ℝ, 0 < tau → ∃ k : ℝ, 0 < k ∧ coffee k 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = tau) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ tau ≤ t)) := by
  have hL : 0 < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.log_pos (by norm_num)
  constructor
  · intro k hk
    exact ⟨initial_temperature k, div_pos hL hk, threshold_time k hk,
      threshold_order k hk⟩
  · intro tau htau
    let k := Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / tau
    have hk : 0 < k := div_pos hL htau
    have htime : Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k = tau := by
      dsimp [k]
      field_simp
    refine ⟨k, hk, initial_temperature k, ?_, ?_⟩
    · intro t
      simpa only [Nat.cast_ofNat, htime] using (threshold_time k hk t)
    · intro t
      simpa only [Nat.cast_ofNat, htime] using (threshold_order k hk t)

    have hx : -k * (Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
      field_simp
    unfold coffee
    rw [hx, threshold_exponential]
    norm_num

-- At or below the target iff the threshold time has passed: this is a first crossing.
lemma threshold_order : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t) := by
  intro k hk t
  calc
    coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.exp (-k * t) ≤ Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) := by
      rw [threshold_exponential]
      unfold coffee
      constructor <;> intro h <;> linarith
    _ ↔ -k * t ≤ -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.exp_le_exp
    _ ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hk]
      constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

/- Scientific answer, conditional on the family above:
(1) Every positive k fits 90 C initially, predicts a positive first hitting time
    log(7/4)/k, and is above 60 C before that time.
(2) For EVERY proposed positive time tau, some positive k gives exactly that
    first hitting time while fitting the same supplied initial temperature.
Thus these data cannot identify even a bounded positive cooling time. -/
theorem answer_theorem :
    (∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k →
      coffee k 0 = 90 ∧
      0 < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t)) ∧
    (∀ tau : ℝ, 0 < tau → ∃ k : ℝ, 0 < k ∧ coffee k 0 = 90 ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = tau) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ tau ≤ t)) := by
  have hL : 0 < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.log_pos (by norm_num)
  constructor
  · intro k hk
    exact ⟨initial_temperature k, div_pos hL hk, threshold_time k hk,
      threshold_order k hk⟩
  · intro tau htau
    let k := Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / tau
    have hk : 0 < k := div_pos hL htau
    have htime : Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k = tau := by
      dsimp [k]
      field_simp
    refine ⟨k, hk, initial_temperature k, ?_, ?_⟩
    · intro t
      rw [threshold_time k hk, htime]
    · intro t
      rw [threshold_order k hk, htime]

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Basic
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.ExpDeriv
import Mathlib.Tactic.NormNum
import Mathlib.Tactic.Linarith
import Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp
import Mathlib.Tactic.Ring
import Mathlib.Tactic.Convert

/- Chosen deterministic scenario: a uniformly mixed coffee in a constant 20 C
room. Time t is in minutes; k is an UNKNOWN effective loss rate in min^-1.
The exponential family is the modeling assumption, not measured evidence.
The factor 70 is the supplied initial 90 C minus the chosen ambient 20 C. -/
noncomputable def coffee (k t : ℝ) : ℝ := 20 + 70 * Real.exp (-k * t)

-- Every rate fits the supplied initial temperature; it contains no rate information.
lemma initial_temperature : ∀ k : ℝ, coffee k 0 = 90 := by
  intro k
  norm_num [coffee]

-- The chosen family satisfies Newton's cooling equation, with no extra premise.
lemma cooling_equation : ∀ k t : ℝ,
    HasDerivAt (coffee k) (-k * (coffee k t - 20)) t := by
  intro k t
  have h := (((hasDerivAt_id t).const_mul (-k)).exp.const_mul 70).const_add 20
  change HasDerivAt (coffee k)
    (70 * (Real.exp (-k * t) * (-k * 1))) t at h
  convert h using 1 <;> dsimp [coffee] <;> ring

lemma threshold_exponential :
    Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
  have h : Real.log (4 / 7 : ℝ) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
    rw [← Real.log_inv]
    norm_num
  rw [← h, Real.exp_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 4 / 7)]

-- Solving the observation equation: 60 C occurs at exactly log(7/4)/k minutes.
lemma threshold_time : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) := by
  intro k hk t
  constructor
  · intro h
    have he : Real.exp (-k * t) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
      dsimp [coffee] at h
      linarith
    have hx : -k * t = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) :=
      Real.exp_injective (he.trans threshold_exponential.symm)
    apply (eq_div_iff (ne_of_gt hk)).2
    nlinarith
  · intro h
    rw [h]
    have hx : -k * (Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
      field_simp
    unfold coffee
    rw [hx, threshold_exponential]
    norm_num

-- At or below the target iff the threshold time has passed: this is a first crossing.
lemma threshold_order : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t) := by
  intro k hk t
  calc
    coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.exp (-k * t) ≤ Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) := by
      rw [threshold_exponential]
      unfold coffee
      constructor <;> intro h <;> linarith
    _ ↔ -k * t ≤ -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.exp_le_exp
    _ ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hk]
      constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

/- Scientific answer, conditional on the family above:
(1) Every positive k fits 90 C initially, predicts a positive first hitting time
    log(7/4)/k, and is above 60 C before that time.
(2) For EVERY proposed positive time tau, some positive k gives exactly that
    first hitting time while fitting the same supplied initial temperature.
Thus these data cannot identify even a bounded positive cooling time. -/
theorem answer_theorem :
    (∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k →
      coffee k 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      0 < Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k ≤ t)) ∧
    (∀ tau : ℝ, 0 < tau → ∃ k : ℝ, 0 < k ∧ coffee k 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = tau) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ tau ≤ t)) := by
  have hL : 0 < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.log_pos (by norm_num)
  constructor
  · intro k hk
    exact ⟨initial_temperature k, div_pos hL hk, threshold_time k hk,
      threshold_order k hk⟩
  · intro tau htau
    let k := Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / tau
    have hk : 0 < k := div_pos hL htau
    have htime : Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k = tau := by
      dsimp [k]
      field_simp
    refine ⟨k, hk, initial_temperature k, ?_, ?_⟩
    · intro t
      simpa only [Nat.cast_ofNat, htime] using (threshold_time k hk t)
    · intro t
      simpa only [Nat.cast_ofNat, htime] using (threshold_order k hk t)

Compact review packet
Supplied information: initial coffee temperature 90 degrees Celsius; desired target 60 degrees Celsius. No ambient temperature, elapsed-temperature reading, or heat-transfer coefficient is supplied. The target is not an observed timed event.

Chosen scenario details, not measurements: an open cylindrical ceramic mug of internal radius 4 cm, filled to depth 5 cm (about 251 mL), in a room held at 20 degrees Celsius. These describe a reasonable cup but do not determine its heat loss. Cup thickness, thermal state, contact with a table, humidity, air movement and evaporation remain unspecified. Geometry is descriptive context and has no numerical role in the conditional inference; the effective cooling rate is left free rather than assigned from geometry.

Independent modeling choices: represent the liquid by a single uniform temperature (e.g. gently mixed); hold ambient at 20 C; approximate total heat loss over 90 to 60 C by a positive, constant effective Newton cooling rate k in inverse minutes, with no additions or active heating. This is a deterministic exponential family, coffee(k,t) = 20 + 70 exp(-k t), for physical times t >= 0. Its algebraic extension to all real times is used in the statements. There is no prior distribution and no assumed rate value. The supplied initial temperature is treated as exact, not as a noisy reading. With only that initial reading there is no observation error distribution to fit and no information about k.

Reason for model: a lumped thermal energy balance C dT/dt = -G(T-20) with constant effective thermal capacitance C and conductance G gives k = G/C (with units converted to minutes). Neither G nor C is inferred here. COMSOL's primary documentation explains the homogeneous-temperature, constant-coefficient lumped approximation and its limitations for solids: https://doc.comsol.com/6.3/doc/com.comsol.help.heat/heat_ug_theory.07.039.html (accessed 2026-11-16). Applying a single-temperature effective model to this mixed liquid is our additional assumption, not a conclusion of that source. The proof verifies that the chosen exponential family satisfies dT/dt = -k(T-20). It does not prove that the real mug obeys the approximation. Radiation, evaporation and cup thermal transients can make a constant k inaccurate. No physical measurement or simulation has been performed.

Necessary definition: coffee(k,t) is the temperature in Celsius in the chosen model. The 70 C excess is derived from 90 minus the chosen 20 C ambient. Natural logarithm and exponential are Mathlib real functions. Cup dimensions are not hidden parameters in this definition.

Proved conclusion (answer_theorem): for every k > 0, the model fits 90 C at time zero and first reaches 60 C at the positive time t60 = ln(7/4)/k. In addition, for every positive proposed time tau, choosing k = ln(7/4)/tau yields a model with the same initial temperature and exactly that first target crossing. All times before the crossing are above 60 C, and all times after it are at or below 60 C. Hence the initial datum alone permits any positive time within this model family; there is no identifiable numerical answer or finite positive range. This nonidentifiability is already present with ambient fixed at the chosen 20 C. It persists when ambient is also unknown.

Practical conditional prediction: t60 = ln(70/40)/k, approximately 0.5596/k minutes if k is expressed in inverse minutes. The decimal is explanatory only; the formal answer uses the exact logarithm. The theorem is about the model and does not assert that arbitrarily short or long times are physically attainable in the chosen ceramic mug. No independent physical bounds on its effective k have been supplied or derived.

What would determine a prediction: measure actual ambient a and one coffee temperature y after a known elapsed time s > 0, keeping the same cup and conditions. If a < y < 90, under the same exact Newton model k = ln((90-a)/(y-a))/s. If also a < 60, then t60 = s ln((90-a)/(60-a))/ln((90-a)/(y-a)). With the chosen a = 20, substitute 20. These calibration formulas describe a future procedure, not incorporated observations or extra formal conclusions. Several timed readings would allow checking model adequacy and quantifying thermometer uncertainty. An ambient at or above 60 would change whether a finite crossing occurs. A room reading and a timed coffee reading have not been invented.

Proof scope and review boundary: no added axioms or sorry are intended. The scientific assumptions are explicit in the definition of coffee and the restriction k > 0. Technical exponential inversion, positivity, initial-temperature fitting, and the free-rate construction are proved, not assumed. The human must assess the real-world scenario and approximation. Formal correctness cannot establish those assumptions. This is a conditional candidate for human review, not human acceptance.

Produced code

checks/48e34df6a80145eeab8384bb77cfbf69/submitted.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Basic
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.ExpDeriv
import Mathlib.Tactic.NormNum
import Mathlib.Tactic.Linarith
import Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp
import Mathlib.Tactic.Ring
import Mathlib.Tactic.Convert

/- Chosen deterministic scenario: a uniformly mixed coffee in a constant 20 C
room. Time t is in minutes; k is an UNKNOWN effective loss rate in min^-1.
The exponential family is the modeling assumption, not measured evidence.
The factor 70 is the supplied initial 90 C minus the chosen ambient 20 C. -/
noncomputable def coffee (k t : ℝ) : ℝ := 20 + 70 * Real.exp (-k * t)

-- Every rate fits the supplied initial temperature; it contains no rate information.
lemma initial_temperature : ∀ k : ℝ, coffee k 0 = 90 := by
  intro k
  norm_num [coffee]

-- The chosen family satisfies Newton's cooling equation, with no extra premise.
lemma cooling_equation : ∀ k t : ℝ,
    HasDerivAt (coffee k) (-k * (coffee k t - 20)) t := by
  intro k t
  have h := (((hasDerivAt_id t).const_mul (-k)).exp.const_mul 70).const_add 20
  change HasDerivAt (coffee k)
    (70 * (Real.exp (-k * t) * (-k * 1))) t at h
  convert h using 1 <;> dsimp [coffee] <;> ring

lemma threshold_exponential :
    Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
  have h : Real.log (4 / 7 : ℝ) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
    rw [← Real.log_inv]
    norm_num
  rw [← h, Real.exp_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 4 / 7)]

-- Solving the observation equation: 60 C occurs at exactly log(7/4)/k minutes.
lemma threshold_time : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) := by
  intro k hk t
  constructor
  · intro h
    have he : Real.exp (-k * t) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
      dsimp [coffee] at h
      linarith
    have hx : -k * t = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) :=
      Real.exp_injective (he.trans threshold_exponential.symm)
    apply (eq_div_iff (ne_of_gt hk)).2
    nlinarith
  · intro h
    rw [h]
    have hx : -k * (Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
      field_simp
    unfold coffee
    rw [hx, threshold_exponential]
    norm_num

-- At or below the target iff the threshold time has passed: this is a first crossing.
lemma threshold_order : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t) := by
  intro k hk t
  calc
    coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.exp (-k * t) ≤ Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) := by
      rw [threshold_exponential]
      unfold coffee
      constructor <;> intro h <;> linarith
    _ ↔ -k * t ≤ -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.exp_le_exp
    _ ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hk]
      constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

/- Scientific answer, conditional on the family above:
(1) Every positive k fits 90 C initially, predicts a positive first hitting time
    log(7/4)/k, and is above 60 C before that time.
(2) For EVERY proposed positive time tau, some positive k gives exactly that
    first hitting time while fitting the same supplied initial temperature.
Thus these data cannot identify even a bounded positive cooling time. -/
theorem answer_theorem :
    (∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k →
      coffee k 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      0 < Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k ≤ t)) ∧
    (∀ tau : ℝ, 0 < tau → ∃ k : ℝ, 0 < k ∧ coffee k 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = tau) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ tau ≤ t)) := by
  have hL : 0 < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.log_pos (by norm_num)
  constructor
  · intro k hk
    exact ⟨initial_temperature k, div_pos hL hk, threshold_time k hk,
      threshold_order k hk⟩
  · intro tau htau
    let k := Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / tau
    have hk : 0 < k := div_pos hL htau
    have htime : Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k = tau := by
      dsimp [k]
      field_simp
    refine ⟨k, hk, initial_temperature k, ?_, ?_⟩
    · intro t
      simpa only [Nat.cast_ofNat, htime] using (threshold_time k hk t)
    · intro t
      simpa only [Nat.cast_ofNat, htime] using (threshold_order k hk t)

import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Basic
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.ExpDeriv
import Mathlib.Tactic.NormNum
import Mathlib.Tactic.Linarith
import Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp
import Mathlib.Tactic.Ring
import Mathlib.Tactic.Convert

/- Chosen deterministic scenario: a uniformly mixed coffee in a constant 20 C
room. Time t is in minutes; k is an UNKNOWN effective loss rate in min^-1.
The exponential family is the modeling assumption, not measured evidence.
The factor 70 is the supplied initial 90 C minus the chosen ambient 20 C. -/
noncomputable def coffee (k t : ℝ) : ℝ := 20 + 70 * Real.exp (-k * t)

-- Every rate fits the supplied initial temperature; it contains no rate information.
lemma initial_temperature : ∀ k : ℝ, coffee k 0 = 90 := by
  intro k
  norm_num [coffee]

-- The chosen family satisfies Newton's cooling equation, with no extra premise.
lemma cooling_equation : ∀ k t : ℝ,
    HasDerivAt (coffee k) (-k * (coffee k t - 20)) t := by
  intro k t
  have h := (((hasDerivAt_id t).const_mul (-k)).exp.const_mul 70).const_add 20
  change HasDerivAt (coffee k)
    (70 * (Real.exp (-k * t) * (-k * 1))) t at h
  convert h using 1 <;> dsimp [coffee] <;> ring

lemma threshold_exponential :
    Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
  have h : Real.log (4 / 7 : ℝ) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
    rw [← Real.log_inv]
    norm_num
  rw [← h, Real.exp_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 4 / 7)]

-- Solving the observation equation: 60 C occurs at exactly log(7/4)/k minutes.
lemma threshold_time : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t = 60 ↔ t = Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) := by
  intro k hk t
  constructor
  · intro h
    have he : Real.exp (-k * t) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
      dsimp [coffee] at h
      linarith
    have hx : -k * t = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) :=
      Real.exp_injective (he.trans threshold_exponential.symm)
    apply (eq_div_iff (ne_of_gt hk)).2
    nlinarith
  · intro h
    rw [h]
    have hx : -k * (Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
      field_simp
    unfold coffee
    rw [hx, threshold_exponential]
    norm_num

-- At or below the target iff the threshold time has passed: this is a first crossing.
lemma threshold_order : ∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k → ∀ t : ℝ,
    (coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t) := by
  intro k hk t
  calc
    coffee k t ≤ 60 ↔ Real.exp (-k * t) ≤ Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) := by
      rw [threshold_exponential]
      unfold coffee
      constructor <;> intro h <;> linarith
    _ ↔ -k * t ≤ -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.exp_le_exp
    _ ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k ≤ t := by
      rw [div_le_iff₀ hk]
      constructor <;> intro h <;> nlinarith

/- Scientific answer, conditional on the family above:
(1) Every positive k fits 90 C initially, predicts a positive first hitting time
    log(7/4)/k, and is above 60 C before that time.
(2) For EVERY proposed positive time tau, some positive k gives exactly that
    first hitting time while fitting the same supplied initial temperature.
Thus these data cannot identify even a bounded positive cooling time. -/
theorem answer_theorem :
    (∀ k : ℝ, 0 < k →
      coffee k 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      0 < Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ Real.log (((7 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ) : ℝ) / k ≤ t)) ∧
    (∀ tau : ℝ, 0 < tau → ∃ k : ℝ, 0 < k ∧ coffee k 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t = ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = tau) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee k t ≤ ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ tau ≤ t)) := by
  have hL : 0 < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.log_pos (by norm_num)
  constructor
  · intro k hk
    exact ⟨initial_temperature k, div_pos hL hk, threshold_time k hk,
      threshold_order k hk⟩
  · intro tau htau
    let k := Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / tau
    have hk : 0 < k := div_pos hL htau
    have htime : Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / k = tau := by
      dsimp [k]
      field_simp
    refine ⟨k, hk, initial_temperature k, ?_, ?_⟩
    · intro t
      simpa only [Nat.cast_ofNat, htime] using (threshold_time k hk t)
    · intro t
      simpa only [Nat.cast_ofNat, htime] using (threshold_order k hk t)

The supplied information does not determine a cooling time.

Compact review packet
Supplied information: initial coffee temperature 90 degrees Celsius; desired target 60 degrees Celsius. No ambient temperature, elapsed-temperature reading, or heat-transfer coefficient is supplied. The target is not an observed timed event.

Chosen scenario details, not measurements: an open cylindrical ceramic mug of internal radius 4 cm, filled to depth 5 cm (about 251 mL), in a room held at 20 degrees Celsius. These describe a reasonable cup but do not determine its heat loss. Cup thickness, thermal state, contact with a table, humidity, air movement and evaporation remain unspecified. Geometry is descriptive context and has no numerical role in the conditional inference; the effective cooling rate is left free rather than assigned from geometry.

Independent modeling choices: represent the liquid by a single uniform temperature (e.g. gently mixed); hold ambient at 20 C; approximate total heat loss over 90 to 60 C by a positive, constant effective Newton cooling rate k in inverse minutes, with no additions or active heating. This is a deterministic exponential family, coffee(k,t) = 20 + 70 exp(-k t), for physical times t >= 0. Its algebraic extension to all real times is used in the statements. There is no prior distribution and no assumed rate value. The supplied initial temperature is treated as exact, not as a noisy reading. With only that initial reading there is no observation error distribution to fit and no information about k.

Reason for model: a lumped thermal energy balance C dT/dt = -G(T-20) with constant effective thermal capacitance C and conductance G gives k = G/C (with units converted to minutes). Neither G nor C is inferred here. COMSOL's primary documentation explains the homogeneous-temperature, constant-coefficient lumped approximation and its limitations for solids: https://doc.comsol.com/6.3/doc/com.comsol.help.heat/heat_ug_theory.07.039.html (accessed 2026-11-16). Applying a single-temperature effective model to this mixed liquid is our additional assumption, not a conclusion of that source. The proof verifies that the chosen exponential family satisfies dT/dt = -k(T-20). It does not prove that the real mug obeys the approximation. Radiation, evaporation and cup thermal transients can make a constant k inaccurate. No physical measurement or simulation has been performed.

Necessary definition: coffee(k,t) is the temperature in Celsius in the chosen model. The 70 C excess is derived from 90 minus the chosen 20 C ambient. Natural logarithm and exponential are Mathlib real functions. Cup dimensions are not hidden parameters in this definition.

Proved conclusion (answer_theorem): for every k > 0, the model fits 90 C at time zero and first reaches 60 C at the positive time t60 = ln(7/4)/k. In addition, for every positive proposed time tau, choosing k = ln(7/4)/tau yields a model with the same initial temperature and exactly that first target crossing. All times before the crossing are above 60 C, and all times after it are at or below 60 C. Hence the initial datum alone permits any positive time within this model family; there is no identifiable numerical answer or finite positive range. This nonidentifiability is already present with ambient fixed at the chosen 20 C. It persists when ambient is also unknown.

Practical conditional prediction: t60 = ln(70/40)/k, approximately 0.5596/k minutes if k is expressed in inverse minutes. The decimal is explanatory only; the formal answer uses the exact logarithm. The theorem is about the model and does not assert that arbitrarily short or long times are physically attainable in the chosen ceramic mug. No independent physical bounds on its effective k have been supplied or derived.

What would determine a prediction: measure actual ambient a and one coffee temperature y after a known elapsed time s > 0, keeping the same cup and conditions. If a < y < 90, under the same exact Newton model k = ln((90-a)/(y-a))/s. If also a < 60, then t60 = s ln((90-a)/(60-a))/ln((90-a)/(y-a)). With the chosen a = 20, substitute 20. These calibration formulas describe a future procedure, not incorporated observations or extra formal conclusions. Several timed readings would allow checking model adequacy and quantifying thermometer uncertainty. An ambient at or above 60 would change whether a finite crossing occurs. A room reading and a timed coffee reading have not been invented.

Proof scope and review boundary: no added axioms or sorry are intended. The scientific assumptions are explicit in the definition of coffee and the restriction k > 0. Technical exponential inversion, positivity, initial-temperature fitting, and the free-rate construction are proved, not assumed. The human must assess the real-world scenario and approximation. Formal correctness cannot establish those assumptions. This is a conditional candidate for human review, not human acceptance.

Lineage: cycle 1, empty /work at start, no previous feedback or input responses present. No prior solutions consulted. The source above motivates the general approximation and supplies no coefficient. Raw harness feedback is retained alongside the candidate.

#!/bin/sh
set -eu
# HOME is fresh tmpfs; Docker creates bind-mount parents as root. Mount only the
# native credential file and symlink it into an independently writable HOME.
if [ -f /native-auth/auth.json ]; then
    mkdir -p "$HOME/.codex"
    ln -s /native-auth/auth.json "$HOME/.codex/auth.json"
fi
if [ -f /native-auth/claude-credentials.json ]; then
    mkdir -p "$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR"
    ln -s /native-auth/claude-credentials.json "$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.credentials.json"
    # Fresh non-account preferences only. Never copy host ~/.claude.json.
    printf '%s\n' '{"hasCompletedOnboarding":true,"bypassPermissionsModeAccepted":true}' > "$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.claude.json"
fi
exec "$@"

assistant
The illustrative calculation gives about **32 minutes**. It assumes roughly 250 mL of coffee in a preheated ceramic mug, a 20 °C room, and an insulated base. Varying the assumed heat capacity and surface heat loss gives roughly **19–61 minutes**. That range describes the chosen scenarios, not a confidence interval for your cup. I’m formalizing the heat balance, first crossing of 60 °C, and numerical bounds.

Real.log_le_sub_one_of_pos {x : ℝ} (hx : 0 < x) : Real.log x ≤ x - 1
Real.log_lt_log {x y : ℝ} (hx : 0 < x) (h : x < y) : Real.log x < Real.log y
Real.log_pow (x : ℝ) (n : ℕ) : Real.log (x ^ n) = ↑n * Real.log x
Real.log_inv (x : ℝ) : Real.log x⁻¹ = -Real.log x
Real.exp_injective : Function.Injective Real.exp

mul_div_assoc.{u_1} {G : Type u_1} [DivInvMonoid G] (a b c : G) : a * b / c = a * (b / c)

attempt-002/Model.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Basic
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.ExpDeriv
import Mathlib.Tactic.NormNum
import Mathlib.Tactic.Linarith
import Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp
import Mathlib.Tactic.Ring
import Mathlib.Tactic.Convert

/- Conditional lumped heat-balance model. C is total heat capacity (J/K),
G total heat-loss conductance (W/K), t minutes, coffee temperature Celsius.
Ambient 20 C is chosen; initial 90 C is supplied. The 60 converts minutes
into seconds. Constant C and G, uniform temperature and initially hot mug
are modeling assumptions, not observations. -/
noncomputable def coffee (C G t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  20 + (90 - 20) * Real.exp (-(60 * G / C) * t)

lemma initial_temperature : ∀ C G : ℝ, coffee C G 0 = 90 := by
  intro C G
  norm_num [coffee]

-- The chosen temperature family satisfies C dT/dt = -60 G (T-20).
lemma heat_balance : ∀ C G t : ℝ, 0 < C →
    HasDerivAt (coffee C G) (-(60 * G / C) * (coffee C G t - 20)) t ∧
    C * (-(60 * G / C) * (coffee C G t - 20)) =
      -60 * G * (coffee C G t - 20) := by
  intro C G t hC
  constructor
  · have h := (((hasDerivAt_id t).const_mul (-(60 * G / C))).exp.const_mul
      (90 - 20)).const_add 20
    change HasDerivAt (coffee C G)
      ((90 - 20) * (Real.exp (-(60 * G / C) * t) * (-(60 * G / C) * 1))) t at h
    convert h using 1 <;> dsimp [coffee] <;> ring
  · field_simp
    <;> ring

lemma threshold_exponential :
    Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
  have h : Real.log (4 / 7 : ℝ) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
    rw [← Real.log_inv]
    norm_num
  rw [← h, Real.exp_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 4 / 7)]

-- Solves the threshold equation and proves it is the first downward crossing.
lemma first_crossing : ∀ C G : ℝ, 0 < C → 0 < G →
    (∀ t : ℝ, coffee C G t = 60 ↔
      t = C * Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / (60 * G)) ∧
    (∀ t : ℝ, coffee C G t ≤ 60 ↔
      C * Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / (60 * G) ≤ t) := by
  intro C G hC hG
  have hk : 0 < 60 * G / C := div_pos (by positivity) hC
  have htime : Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / (60 * G / C) =
      C * Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / (60 * G) := by field_simp
  constructor
  · intro t
    constructor
    · intro h
      have he : Real.exp (-(60 * G / C) * t) = (4 / 7 : ℝ) := by
        dsimp [coffee] at h
        linarith
      have hx := Real.exp_injective (he.trans threshold_exponential.symm)
      rw [← htime]
      apply (eq_div_iff (ne_of_gt hk)).2
      linarith
    · intro h
      rw [← htime] at h
      rw [h]
      have hx : -(60 * G / C) * (Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / (60 * G / C)) =
          -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by field_simp
      unfold coffee
      rw [hx, threshold_exponential]
      norm_num
  · intro t
    rw [← htime]
    calc
      coffee C G t ≤ 60 ↔
        Real.exp (-(60 * G / C) * t) ≤ Real.exp (-Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ)) := by
          rw [threshold_exponential]
          unfold coffee
          constructor <;> intro h <;> linarith
      _ ↔ -(60 * G / C) * t ≤ -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := Real.exp_le_exp
      _ ↔ Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / (60 * G / C) ≤ t := by
        rw [div_le_iff₀ hk]
        constructor <;> intro h <;> linarith

-- Certified logarithm bounds, derived from log x <= x-1 and monotonicity.
-- No decimal logarithm or numerical cooling time is assumed.
lemma logarithm_bounds : (55 / 100 : ℝ) < Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) ∧
    Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) < (57 / 100 : ℝ) := by
  have hu := Real.log_lt_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 7 / 4)
    (by norm_num : (7 / 4 : ℝ) < (1019 / 1000 : ℝ) ^ 30)
  have hu' := Real.log_le_sub_one_of_pos (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 1019 / 1000)
  rw [Real.log_pow] at hu
  have hl := Real.log_lt_log (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 4 / 7)
    (by norm_num : (4 / 7 : ℝ) < (589 / 600 : ℝ) ^ 30)
  have hl' := Real.log_le_sub_one_of_pos (by norm_num : (0 : ℝ) < 589 / 600)
  rw [Real.log_pow] at hl
  have hi : Real.log (4 / 7 : ℝ) = -Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) := by
    rw [← Real.log_inv]
    norm_num
  rw [hi] at hl
  constructor <;> linarith

/- Scientific uncertainty result. Rounded exposed side area 1/80 m^2 and
open surface area 1/200 m^2 are chosen for a ~250 mL cylinder, r~4 cm,
fill depth 5 cm. hs includes side convection+radiation+wall resistance;
ht includes top convection+radiation+evaporation. Base is insulated.
Assumed scenario ranges: C 1100..1300 J/K, hs 8..16, ht 20..70 W/m^2/K.
Conclusion: in every such constant-coefficient model, first 60 C crossing
is strictly between 18 and 62 minutes. These are NOT empirical bounds. -/
theorem scenario_answer :
    ∀ C hs ht : ℝ,
    ((1100 : ℕ) : ℝ) ≤ C → C ≤ ((1300 : ℕ) : ℝ) →
    ((8 : ℕ) : ℝ) ≤ hs → hs ≤ ((16 : ℕ) : ℝ) →
    ((20 : ℕ) : ℝ) ≤ ht → ht ≤ ((70 : ℕ) : ℝ) →
    ∃ tau : ℝ, ((18 : ℕ) : ℝ) < tau ∧ tau < ((62 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      coffee C (hs / ((80 : ℕ) : ℝ) + ht / ((200 : ℕ) : ℝ)) 0 = ((90 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee C (hs / ((80 : ℕ) : ℝ) + ht / ((200 : ℕ) : ℝ)) t =
        ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = tau) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ, coffee C (hs / ((80 : ℕ) : ℝ) + ht / ((200 : ℕ) : ℝ)) t ≤
        ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ tau ≤ t) := by
  intro C hs ht hClo hChi hslo hshi htlo hthi
  norm_num at hClo hChi hslo hshi htlo hthi
  let G := hs / 80 + ht / 200
  have hGlo : (1 / 5 : ℝ) ≤ G := by dsimp [G]; linarith
  have hGhi : G ≤ (11 / 20 : ℝ) := by dsimp [G]; linarith
  have hC : 0 < C := by linarith
  have hG : 0 < G := by linarith
  have hden : 0 < 60 * G := by positivity
  obtain ⟨hLlo, hLhi⟩ := logarithm_bounds
  let tau := C * Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / (60 * G)
  have hlo : 18 < tau := by
    dsimp [tau]
    rw [lt_div_iff₀ hden]
    have hprod : C * (55 / 100) < C * Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) :=
      mul_lt_mul_of_pos_left hLlo hC
    nlinarith
  have hhi : tau < 62 := by
    dsimp [tau]
    rw [div_lt_iff₀ hden]
    have hprod : C * Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) < C * (57 / 100) :=
      mul_lt_mul_of_pos_left hLhi hC
    nlinarith
  exact ⟨tau, hlo, hhi, initial_temperature C G,
    (first_crossing C G hC hG).1, (first_crossing C G hC hG).2⟩

/- Worked nominal scenario: capacity = 0.25 kg *4200 J/kg/K water-like
coffee + 0.20 kg *800 J/kg/K chosen ceramic. hs=14, ht=35 W/m^2/K.
All these physical parameters are scenario assumptions, not fitted data.
Conclusion: first crossing is between 31 and 33 minutes. -/
theorem nominal_answer :
    ∃ tau : ℝ, ((31 : ℕ) : ℝ) < tau ∧ tau < ((33 : ℕ) : ℝ) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ,
        coffee ((1 / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ)) * ((4200 : ℕ) : ℝ) +
          (1 / ((5 : ℕ) : ℝ)) * ((800 : ℕ) : ℝ))
          (((14 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((80 : ℕ) : ℝ) + ((35 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((200 : ℕ) : ℝ)) t =
          ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ t = tau) ∧
      (∀ t : ℝ,
        coffee ((1 / ((4 : ℕ) : ℝ)) * ((4200 : ℕ) : ℝ) +
          (1 / ((5 : ℕ) : ℝ)) * ((800 : ℕ) : ℝ))
          (((14 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((80 : ℕ) : ℝ) + ((35 : ℕ) : ℝ) / ((200 : ℕ) : ℝ)) t ≤
          ((60 : ℕ) : ℝ) ↔ tau ≤ t) := by
  have hC : (0 : ℝ) < 1210 := by norm_num
  have hG : (0 : ℝ) < 7 / 20 := by norm_num
  have hcross := first_crossing 1210 (7 / 20) hC hG
  obtain ⟨hlo, hhi⟩ := logarithm_bounds
  refine ⟨1210 * Real.log (7 / 4 : ℝ) / 21, ?_, ?_, ?_, ?_⟩
  · nlinarith
  · nlinarith
  · norm_num at hcross ⊢
    exact hcross.1
  · norm_num at hcross ⊢
    exact hcross.2
attempt-002/extracted.lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.SpecialFunctions.Log.Basic

import Mathlib.Tactic.NormNum
import Mathlib.Tactic.Linarith
import Mathlib.Tactic.FieldSimp
import Mathlib.Tactic.Ring
import Mathlib.Tactic.Convert

noncomputable def coffee (C G t : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  20 + (90 - 20) * Real.exp (-(60 * G / C) * t)


/-! Conclusions (statements of the proved theorems). -/
